I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, but I don’t feel unusually energized from it. I used to plan my week around things like salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Lately I mostly cancel, and then I just sit at home scrolling on my phone or pretending to watch a show.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in your energy level or felt fatigued lately, even when you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had some low days, but I haven’t really been tracking it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days in a row where you felt down or lost interest in things, and if so, how many days in a row would that be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have had stretches like that. Now I mostly cancel plans and stay home, scrolling on my phone or just pretending to watch a show.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks straight without improvement?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure it was exactly two weeks straight. I used to plan my week around salsa, book club, yoga, or cooking for people, and lately I just cancel things and sit at home scrolling or half-watching TV. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of out of it sometimes, but not like I’m outside my body or like the world isn’t real.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was true that others didn’t seem to agree with, or when you felt like you were seeing or understanding things that others couldn’t?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t had a sense that I know things other people can’t, or that I’m seeing something they’re not. I’ve been stressed, but not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing faster than usual?\n\nPatient: Not really. I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, but I don’t feel unusually energized from it. And I haven’t noticed my thoughts racing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down, depressed, or hopeless for most of the day, nearly every day, or when you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more the losing interest part. I used to plan my week around things like salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Lately I mostly cancel, and then I just sit at home scrolling on my phone or pretending to watch a show.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in your energy level or felt fatigued lately, even when you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but I haven’t noticed a big drop in my energy where I feel exhausted all the time or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to focus or concentrate lately, like trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get distracted like anyone, but I haven’t noticed a real change in my focus or decision-making lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say depressed or hopeless most days for two weeks straight. I’ve had some low days, but I haven’t really been tracking it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days in a row where you felt down or lost interest in things, and if so, how many days in a row would that be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have had stretches like that. I’m not sure exactly how many days in a row, but sometimes it’s several days where I just don’t want to do anything.\n\nI used to plan my week around salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Now I mostly cancel plans and stay home, scrolling on my phone or just pretending to watch a show.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks straight without improvement?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure it was exactly two weeks straight. But it has felt like more than just a day or two. I used to plan my week around salsa, book club, yoga, or cooking for people, and lately I just cancel things and sit at home scrolling or half-watching TV. So… yes, there have been stretches where it doesn’t really lift much.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that there were times when you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more without much improvement?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure in that exact way—like, I didn’t mark it on a calendar. But yes, there have been stretches where most days I just don’t feel like doing the things I used to enjoy.\n\nI used to really look forward to salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Lately I mostly cancel and stay home, scrolling on my phone or pretending to watch something. So it feels like it has gone on for a while without really lifting much.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there were times when you felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure I can say “two weeks or more” for certain. I know I’ve had stretches where I felt low most of the day and it was happening most days, but I wasn’t really tracking how long it lasted exactly.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say there was a period when you felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I know there have been periods where I felt low most of the day and it happened a lot, but I didn’t really keep track closely enough to say it was definitely two weeks in a row.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F33.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’ve started doing things that honestly scare me. Like, I’ll walk out in the middle of deployments at work, or I’ll drive around late at night with no plan. That’s not how I usually handle stress, and it’s starting to worry me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were watching yourself from outside your body or experienced the world as unreal?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of out of it sometimes, but not like I’m outside my body or like the world isn’t real.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was true that others didn’t seem to agree with, or when you felt like you were seeing or understanding things that others couldn’t?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t had a sense that I know things other people can’t, or that I’m seeing something they’re not. I’ve been stressed, but not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing faster than usual?\n\nPatient: Not really. I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, but I don’t feel unusually energized from it. And I haven’t noticed my thoughts racing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down, depressed, or hopeless for most of the day, nearly every day, or when you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more the losing interest part. I used to plan my week around things like salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Lately I mostly cancel, and then I just sit at home scrolling on my phone or pretending to watch a show.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in your energy level or felt fatigued lately, even when you’re not doing much?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but I haven’t noticed a big drop in my energy where I feel exhausted all the time or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to focus or concentrate lately, like trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get distracted like anyone, but I haven’t noticed a real change in my focus or decision-making lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say depressed or hopeless most days for two weeks straight. I’ve had some low days, but I haven’t really been tracking it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days in a row where you felt down or lost interest in things, and if so, how many days in a row would that be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have had stretches like that. I’m not sure exactly how many days in a row, but sometimes it’s several days where I just don’t want to do anything.\n\nI used to plan my week around salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Now I mostly cancel plans and stay home, scrolling on my phone or just pretending to watch a show.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks straight without improvement?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure it was exactly two weeks straight. But it has felt like more than just a day or two. I used to plan my week around salsa, book club, yoga, or cooking for people, and lately I just cancel things and sit at home scrolling or half-watching TV. So… yes, there have been stretches where it doesn’t really lift much.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that there were times when you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more without much improvement?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure in that exact way—like, I didn’t mark it on a calendar. But yes, there have been stretches where most days I just don’t feel like doing the things I used to enjoy.\n\nI used to really look forward to salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Lately I mostly cancel and stay home, scrolling on my phone or pretending to watch something. So it feels like it has gone on for a while without really lifting much.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there were times when you felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure I can say “two weeks or more” for certain. I know I’ve had stretches where I felt low most of the day and it was happening most days, but I wasn’t really tracking how long it lasted exactly.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say there was a period when you felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I know there have been periods where I felt low most of the day and it happened a lot, but I didn’t really keep track closely enough to say it was definitely two weeks in a row.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say there was a time when you felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I still can’t say for sure. Looking back, there were definitely times I felt low most of the day and it seemed to happen a lot, but I don’t want to say it was definitely two weeks straight if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, was there a time when you felt down or low nearly every day for at least two weeks without much relief?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know if I can say that. I’ve had times where I felt pretty low for a while, but whether it was nearly every day for a full two weeks without much relief… I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but I haven’t noticed a big drop in my energy where I feel exhausted all the time or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to focus or concentrate lately, like trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. But yes, there have been stretches where most days I just don’t feel like doing the things I used to enjoy.\n\nI used to really look forward to salsa, book club, yoga, or having people over for Guatemalan food. Lately I mostly cancel and stay home, scrolling on my phone or pretending to watch something. So it feels like it has gone on for a while without really lifting much.\n\nDoctor: Would you say there were times when you felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure I can say “two weeks or more” for certain. I know I’ve had stretches where I felt low most of the day and it was happening most days, but I wasn’t really tracking how long it lasted exactly.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say there was a period when you felt down or low most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly.
